21	  All four human-verification items exercised on a live Windows/WinFsp build.

23	
24	## Tests
25	
26	### 1. Journal survival after SIGKILL
27	
28	expected: Copy a file into the vault, SIGKILL desktop before upload completes, relaunch. File replays on mount and is present remotely; the cb-journal entry disappears after successful replay.
29	result: PASS
30	evidence: |
31	  Copied a 20 MB file into C:\Users\<user>\CipherBox, then `taskkill /F` on the desktop
32	  immediately after the copy ack'd. The journal entry (9552611c…json, 26.6 MB of base64
33	  ciphertext) was already fsync'd to disk before the kill — confirming release() journals
34	  before acking. On relaunch the log shows `replay_for_vault: UploadFile 9552611c… ('uat1-mid.bin')
35	  replayed successfully`, the journal entry was removed, and the file is present in the mount
36	  with byte-identical content to the original (cmp -s passed). 
37	  Note: the live root listing took one ~30 s sync-poll cycle to show the replayed file and the
38	  readdir cache briefly reported size 0 before a per-file getattr resolved 20,000,000 bytes —
39	  eventual-consistency in the directory cache, not a data-integrity problem.
40	  Aside: an initial attempt with a 120 MB file surfaced that the API caps uploads at 100 MB
41	  (HTTP 413); replay correctly retried and RETAINED the entry ("will retry on next mount"),
42	  which independently confirms the retry-retention branch.
43	
44	### 2. Park notification render
45	
46	expected: Force upload failure, copy a file, let retries exhaust. An OS notification with the failed-upload count appears, the tray shows the WriteParked status, and the journal entry remains on disk with Failed status.
47	result: PASS (after fix — see "Fixes applied" / G-43-UAT-01)
48	evidence: |
49	  First run FAILED on a blocking integration gap: the SyncDaemon that emits WriteParked was
50	  never started (`start_sync_daemon` had zero call sites — see G-43-UAT-01). The journal park
51	  transition itself already worked: a >100 MB file (persistent HTTP 413) with the retry counter
52	  at max_retries(5) parked on the next replay — `replay_for_vault: UploadFile … parked as Failed
53	  after 5 retries: … 413`, on-disk status `Failed{last_error:"…413…"}` — but no notification
54	  could render because nothing turned the daemon on.
55	  After the fix (auto-start the daemon from complete_auth_setup), the retest passed end-to-end:
56	  the log shows `Filesystem mounted …` → `Starting background sync daemon` → `Sync daemon spawned`
57	  → `Sync daemon started (interval: 30s)`; the parked entry reached `Failed` on disk; the daemon's
58	  poll reached `Sync cycle complete — checking journal for parked writes` (failed=1); and the
59	  Windows OS toast "CipherBox Upload Failed — 1 pending upload(s) failed and require attention."
60	  rendered (visually confirmed by the user), with the tray set to WriteParked. All three
61	  sub-assertions (OS notification + tray WriteParked + on-disk Failed entry) now hold.
62	  Note: the retry counter was fast-forwarded to 5 to avoid five real remounts; the per-attempt
63	  increment and park-at-max transition are independently covered by record_failure + its unit test.
64	
65	### 3. Mkdir orphan survival
66	
67	expected: mkdir under a parent with an induced parent-publish conflict; the folder survives an app restart, the parent publishes correctly on retry/replay, and no orphan remains.
68	result: PASS (crash-before-publish path); live-conflict trigger not artificially induced
69	evidence: |
70	  `mkdir C:\Users\<user>\CipherBox\uat3-folder` then immediate `taskkill /F` before the 1.5 s
71	  debounced publish. The journal held a MkdirPublish entry (5bc7b18e…json, status Pending,
72	  folder uat3-folder) — mkdir is journaled before the publish. On relaunch:
73	  `replay_for_vault: MkdirPublish 5bc7b18e… replayed successfully`, the journal entry was removed,
74	  and uat3-folder is present in the mount as a valid directory after restart — no orphan.
75	  The specific trigger tested was crash-before-publish (the durability path the journal protects).
76	  The live in-session MkdirConflict re-arm path (a real IPNS sequence conflict) was not
77	  artificially induced at runtime; it remains code-verified (D-11a, lib.rs:686-691) only.
78	
79	### 4. Ciphertext-only journal check
80	
81	expected: Open any cb-journal/*.json file; it contains only base64/hex ciphertext, wrapped keys, IVs, and IPNS names — never readable file content or plaintext paths.
82	result: ISSUE (content protected; plaintext filenames/sizes present) — see Gaps
83	evidence: |
84	  Inspected real entries created during tests 1–3. File CONTENT is encrypted: `ciphertext_b64`
85	  is AES-GCM ciphertext (not the known plaintext), `wrapped_key_hex` is ECIES-wrapped, `iv_hex`
86	  is the IV, and IPNS names/keys are hex/CID strings — all as expected.
87	  BUT each UploadFile entry stores `"filename":"uat-probe.txt"` and `"size":21` in PLAINTEXT, and
88	  each MkdirPublish entry stores the folder name (`uat3-folder`) in plaintext. The struct field
89	  (crates/sdk/src/queue.rs:43-44, doc-commented "Original filename") is written verbatim. The
90	  D-05 unit test `journal_no_plaintext` (queue.rs:431) only asserts the absence of file CONTENT
91	  and of the literal keys "plaintext"/"parent_ino" — it does NOT assert filenames are absent.
92	  So the strict UAT wording "never … plaintext paths" is not met: file/folder NAMES and sizes
93	  are readable in the on-disk journal (local to the user's device).

104	## Fixes applied
105	
106	- **G-43-UAT-01 fix (commits pending): auto-start the sync daemon after mount.** Extracted the
107	  daemon-spawn logic from the `start_sync_daemon` IPC command into a reusable
108	  `commands::sync::spawn_sync_daemon(app, &AppState)` and call it from `complete_auth_setup`'s
109	  mount-success arm (apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/commands/auth.rs). Because every auth flow
110	  (OAuth, email, session-restore, dev-key test-login) funnels through `complete_auth_setup` and
111	  it is the only `mount_filesystem` call site, this single point guarantees the daemon runs.
112	  Verified by retest: daemon start logs appear and the park notification renders (Test 2 PASS).
113	
114	## Gaps
115	
116	- **G-43-UAT-01 (RESOLVED — Test 2 now PASS): SyncDaemon was never started.** `start_sync_daemon`
117	  had no caller (frontend or Rust), so parked writes never surfaced. Fixed by auto-starting the
118	  daemon from `complete_auth_setup` after a successful mount (see "Fixes applied"). Retest
119	  confirmed the WriteParked path fires and the OS toast renders.
120	
121	- **G-43-UAT-02 (Test 4): Journal stores plaintext filenames/folder names and sizes.** File
122	  content, keys, and IVs are encrypted, but `filename`/folder name and `size` are persisted in
123	  cleartext in cb-journal/*.json. Triage against the threat model: if local-only plaintext names
124	  are acceptable, update the D-05 invariant + UAT wording to say "no plaintext file *content*";
125	  otherwise encrypt the name/size fields. Either way the current behavior contradicts the
126	  test-as-written.
127	
128	- **OBSERVATION (from Test 2 setup): a file whose content upload fails (413) is still listed in
129	  the parent folder.** `uat2-big.bin` appeared in the mount listing even though its content was
130	  never stored (only the parent metadata published, not the content) — a listed-but-unreadable
131	  entry. Worth confirming whether parent-listing publish should be gated on content-upload
132	  success to avoid transient ghost entries.
133	
134	## Notes
135	
136	- Build/run friction encountered (environment, not phase-43 code): local API requires Node ≥20.19/≥22
137	  (jose@6 is ESM-only) — Node was upgraded to 22; stale `packages/*/dist` had to be rebuilt
138	  (the libp2p-crypto v5 ESM bump broke the old CJS crypto bundle); CORS had to allow the Tauri
139	  webview origins (http://localhost:1420 + tauri://localhost / tauri.localhost) — added to
140	  apps/api/.env and .env.example; and the Windows desktop build needs
141	  `--no-default-features --features winfsp` (default `fuse` feature pulls unix libfuse).
142	
```

{
  "name": "@cipherbox/mock-ipns-routing",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "description": "Mock delegated routing service for E2E testing",
  "type": "module",

    "start": "node dist/index.js",
    "dev": "tsx src/index.ts"
  },

    "pino-pretty": "^13.1.3"

=== docker-compose.yml ===
name: cipherbox-infrastructure
services:
  postgres:
    image: postgres:16-alpine
    container_name: cipherbox-postgres
    restart: unless-stopped
    environment:
      POSTGRES_USER: ${DB_USERNAME:-postgres}
      POSTGRES_PASSWORD: ${DB_PASSWORD:-postgres}
      POSTGRES_DB: ${DB_DATABASE:-cipherbox}
    ports:
      - '${DB_PORT:-5432}:5432'
    volumes:
      - postgres_data:/var/lib/postgresql/data
    healthcheck:
      test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'pg_isready -U postgres']
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5

  ipfs:
    image: ipfs/kubo:v0.40.0
    container_name: cipherbox-ipfs
    restart: unless-stopped
    environment:
      - IPFS_PATH=/data/ipfs
      # pebbleds: LSM-tree datastore for faster concurrent write throughput (Phase 19.2)
      # Requires fresh IPFS repo -- delete ipfs_data volume before first start:
      #   docker compose down -v --remove-orphans && docker compose up -d
      - IPFS_PROFILE=server,pebbleds
    volumes:
      - ipfs_data:/data/ipfs
    ports:
      # Swarm - P2P connections
      - '4001:4001/tcp'
      - '4001:4001/udp'
      # TODO: Create environment-specific configs for staging/prod with:
      #   - 127.0.0.1 bindings or reverse proxy with authentication
      #   - IPFS API auth enabled
      # API - bound to all interfaces for dev (VM/host access)
      - '5001:5001'
      # Gateway - bound to all interfaces for dev (VM/host access)
      - '8080:8080'
    healthcheck:
      test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'ipfs id || exit 1']
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 10
      start_period: 30s
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 2G
          cpus: '1.0'

  redis:
    image: redis:7-alpine
    container_name: cipherbox-redis
    restart: unless-stopped
    ports:
      - '${REDIS_PORT:-6380}:6379'
    volumes:
      - redis_data:/data
    healthcheck:
      test: ['CMD', 'redis-cli', 'ping']
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5

  someguy:
    image: ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1
    container_name: cipherbox-someguy
    restart: unless-stopped
    environment:
      SOMEGUY_LISTEN_ADDRESS: 0.0.0.0:8190
      SOMEGUY_DHT: accelerated
      SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_LOW: 100
      SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_CONNMGR_HIGH: 3000
      SOMEGUY_LIBP2P_MAX_MEMORY: 1073741824
      GOLOG_LOG_LEVEL: info
      GOLOG_LOG_FMT: json
    ports:
      # HTTP API for delegated routing
      - '8190:8190'
      # libp2p swarm - required for DHT participation
      - '4004:4004/tcp'
      - '4004:4004/udp'
    healthcheck:
      test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'curl -sf http://localhost:8190/version || exit 1']
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 10
      start_period: 60s
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 2G
          cpus: '1.0'

  mock-ipns-routing:
    build:
      context: ../tools/mock-ipns-routing
      dockerfile: Dockerfile
    container_name: cipherbox-mock-ipns-routing
    restart: unless-stopped
    environment:
      - HOST=0.0.0.0
      - PORT=3001
      - LOG_LEVEL=info
    ports:
      - '127.0.0.1:3001:3001'
    healthcheck:
      test: ['CMD-SHELL', 'wget -q --spider http://localhost:3001/health || exit 1']
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 5

volumes:
  postgres_data:
  ipfs_data:
  redis_data:
```

=== DEVELOPMENT.md (first 200 lines) ===
# Development Guide

## Prerequisites

- **Node.js** 20+
- **pnpm** 9+
- **Docker** (for PostgreSQL, IPFS, Redis)
- **Rust** toolchain (desktop app only)

## Infrastructure

Start the required services:

```bash
docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml up -d
```

This starts the `cipherbox-infrastructure` compose project (containers are named `cipherbox-<service>`):

| Service             | Image                                 | Host Port(s)                                     | Purpose                                  |
| :------------------ | :------------------------------------ | :----------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- |
| `postgres`          | `postgres:16-alpine`                  | 5432 (`DB_PORT`)                                 | Database                                 |
| `ipfs`              | `ipfs/kubo:v0.40.0`                   | 5001 (API), 8080 (gateway), 4001 tcp/udp (swarm) | Decentralized storage (Kubo)             |
| `redis`             | `redis:7-alpine`                      | 6380 (`REDIS_PORT`) → container 6379             | BullMQ job queue                         |
| `someguy`           | `ghcr.io/ipfs/someguy:v0.11.1`        | 8190 (routing API), 4004 tcp/udp (libp2p swarm)  | Delegated IPFS routing (accelerated DHT) |
| `mock-ipns-routing` | built from `tools/mock-ipns-routing/` | 3001 (loopback only)                             | Local IPNS resolution for dev/E2E        |

Notes:

- The IPFS node runs with the `server,pebbleds` datastore profile. If you have an `ipfs_data` volume created before the pebbleds switch, recreate it first: `docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml down -v --remove-orphans`.
- The `ipfs` and `someguy` containers are each capped at 2 GB memory / 1 CPU.
- The staging stack runs a different set of containers (adds `api`, `tee-worker`, `caddy`, `alloy`; drops `mock-ipns-routing`) — see [DEPLOYMENT.md](DEPLOYMENT.md).

## Environment

Copy the example env files:

```bash
cp apps/api/.env.example apps/api/.env
cp apps/web/.env.example apps/web/.env
```

### API (`apps/api/.env`)

Key variables:

| Variable               | Default                 | Notes                                   |
| :--------------------- | :---------------------- | :-------------------------------------- |
| `DB_HOST`              | `localhost`             | PostgreSQL host                         |
| `DB_PORT`              | `5432`                  | PostgreSQL port                         |
| `JWT_SECRET`           | —                       | Required, any random string             |
| `REDIS_HOST`           | `localhost`             | Redis host                              |
| `REDIS_PORT`           | `6380`                  | Redis port (mapped from container 6379) |
| `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` | `http://localhost:5173` | Frontend origin                         |

### Web (`apps/web/.env`)

| Variable                  | Default                    | Notes               |
| :------------------------ | :------------------------- | :------------------ |
| `VITE_WEB3AUTH_CLIENT_ID` | Provided in `.env.example` | Web3Auth project ID |
| `VITE_API_URL`            | `http://localhost:3000`    | API endpoint        |

## Running the Web App

```bash
# Install dependencies (first time)
pnpm install

# Start API + web concurrently
pnpm dev
```

- API: <http://localhost:3000>
- Web: <http://localhost:5173>

Or run individually:

```bash
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api dev    # API only
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web dev    # Web only
```

## Running the Desktop App

### Additional prerequisites

- **macOS:** [FUSE-T](https://www.fuse-t.org/) (`brew install macos-fuse-t/homebrew-cask/fuse-t`)
- **Windows:** [WinFSP](https://winfsp.dev/)
- **Linux:** `libfuse3-dev` (or equivalent)

```bash
cp apps/desktop/.env.example apps/desktop/.env
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/desktop dev
```

The desktop app defaults to the staging API. For local development, update `apps/desktop/.env`:

```bash
VITE_API_URL=http://localhost:3000
VITE_ENVIRONMENT=local
```

The Rust backend also needs the local API URL. Either set it in your shell or prefix the dev command:

```bash
CIPHERBOX_API_URL=http://localhost:3000 pnpm --filter @cipherbox/desktop dev
```

See [apps/desktop/CLAUDE.md](../apps/desktop/CLAUDE.md) for FUSE architecture details and dev-key mode.

## Testing

### Unit tests

```bash
# Run all unit tests (excludes E2E)
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/api test
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/web test
pnpm --filter @cipherbox/crypto test
```

> **Note:** `pnpm test` runs tests across all workspaces including E2E — use the filtered commands above for unit tests only.

### E2E tests (Playwright)

Playwright auto-starts API + web via `webServer` config (requires infra services: Postgres, IPFS, Redis):

```bash
pnpm test:web-e2e
```

Headed mode (shows browser):

```bash
pnpm test:web-e2e:headed
```

### Desktop E2E

```bash
cd tests/desktop-e2e
pnpm exec playwright test
```

## API Client Generation

After modifying API endpoints, DTOs, or controllers, regenerate the typed client to keep the web app in sync:

```bash
pnpm api:generate
```

This generates the OpenAPI spec from the API, creates the typed client at `packages/api-client/`, and runs lint fixes. Always commit the regenerated files with your API changes.

## Code Quality

- **Linting:** `pnpm lint` (ESLint) / `pnpm lint:fix`
- **Markdown:** `pnpm lint:md` / `pnpm lint:md:fix`
- **Type checking:** `pnpm typecheck`
- **Formatting:** Prettier (runs via lint-staged on commit)
- **Commits:** [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) enforced by commitlint (`feat:`, `fix:`, `docs:`, etc.)

## Running the TEE Worker (Simulator Mode)

The TEE worker (`apps/tee-worker`) republishes IPNS records. In production it runs inside a Phala Cloud CVM; locally it runs in **simulator mode**, which uses a deterministic HKDF-SHA256 seed instead of hardware-backed key derivation.

Set the required environment variables and start the worker:

```bash
TEE_MODE=simulator TEE_WORKER_SECRET=dev-secret pnpm --filter cipherbox-tee-worker dev
```

The worker listens on port `3001` by default. Note that the `mock-ipns-routing` container also binds `127.0.0.1:3001` — when running the worker alongside the local Docker infrastructure, pick a free port (e.g. `PORT=3002`) and point `TEE_WORKER_URL` at it. The API authenticates to the TEE worker using a shared `Bearer` token — set the same value in `apps/api/.env`:

```bash
TEE_WORKER_SECRET=dev-secret
TEE_WORKER_URL=http://localhost:3001
```

Key variables:

| Variable            | Required | Notes                                           |
| :------------------ | :------- | :---------------------------------------------- |
| `TEE_MODE`          | Yes      | `simulator` (local) or `cvm` (Phala Cloud prod) |
| `TEE_WORKER_SECRET` | Yes      | Shared secret for Bearer token auth             |
| `PORT`              | No       | Defaults to `3001`                              |

`TEE_MODE=simulator` is blocked at runtime if `NODE_ENV=production` or `CIPHERBOX_ENVIRONMENT=production` to prevent accidental use of the fixed seed in production.
```

=== api/.env.example ===
# Environment
NODE_ENV=development

# Database (PostgreSQL)
DB_HOST=localhost
DB_PORT=5432
DB_USERNAME=postgres
DB_PASSWORD=REDACTED
DB_DATABASE=cipherbox

# JWT Authentication
JWT_SECRET=

# Google OAuth (required in production for audience validation)
# REDACTED.apps.googleusercontent.com

# Identity JWT signing key (RS256 PKCS8 PEM, base64-encoded)
# Required in production. In dev/staging, an ephemeral keypair is generated.
# IDENTITY_JWT_PRIVATE_KEY=

# CORS - allowed origins (comma-separated, supports wildcards with *)
# Also used for SIWE domain validation (non-wildcard entries only)
# Example: https://app.cipherbox.cc,https://cipher-box-pr-*.onrender.com
# Includes the web dev server (5173), the Tauri desktop dev webview (1420),
# and the bundled Tauri webview origins (tauri://localhost on macOS/Linux,
# http(s)://tauri.localhost on Windows) so the desktop app's dev-key/test-login
# flow can reach a local API.
CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS=http://localhost:5173,http://localhost:1420,tauri://localhost,http://tauri.localhost,https://tauri.localhost

# IPFS (Kubo node)
# IPFS_LOCAL_API_URL=http://localhost:5001
# IPFS_LOCAL_GATEWAY_URL=http://localhost:8080

# IPNS Delegated Routing URL
# For production/staging: http://someguy:8190 (self-hosted Someguy sidecar)
# For local/E2E testing: http://localhost:3001 (mock service)
# Legacy: https://delegated-ipfs.dev (public, unreliable -- replaced by self-hosted Someguy)
# DELEGATED_ROUTING_URL=http://localhost:3001

# Fallback routing URL (optional) — used when primary fails after all retries.
# For staging/production with self-hosted Someguy, set to public endpoint as safety net.
# Metrics: cipherbox_delegated_routing_fallbacks_total tracks how often fallback is triggered.
# DELEGATED_ROUTING_FALLBACK_URL=https://delegated-ipfs.dev

# Redis (required for BullMQ job scheduling)

```
1	# Desktop App (`apps/desktop`) - Development Notes
2	
3	## Default API Target
4	
5	The desktop app defaults to the **staging API** (`https://api-staging.cipherbox.cc`). Most development and testing tasks can be completed against staging without running a local API.
6	
7	To develop against the local API instead, update `.env`:
8	
9	```env
10	VITE_API_URL=http://localhost:3000
11	VITE_ENVIRONMENT=local
12	```
13	
14	Also set `CIPHERBOX_API_URL=http://localhost:3000` in the Rust env (used by the native backend):
15	
16	```bash
17	CIPHERBOX_API_URL=http://localhost:3000 pnpm --filter desktop dev
18	```
19	
20	## Running the Desktop App
21	
22	```bash
23	pnpm --filter desktop dev
24	```
25	
26	Vite env vars are loaded from `apps/desktop/.env` automatically. No need to pass them on the command line.
27	
28	## Dev-Key Mode (Headless Auth for Debugging)
29	
30	Debug builds accept `--dev-key <hex>` to bypass Web3Auth login entirely, enabling fast restart cycles during FUSE/backend debugging.
31	
32	**How it works:**
33	
34	1. Pass a 64-char hex secp256k1 private key via `--dev-key` (value is ignored but triggers headless mode)
35	2. The webview detects it via the `get_dev_key` IPC command
36	3. Calls `POST /auth/test-login` with the configured `VITE_TEST_LOGIN_SECRET`
37	4. Gets `{ accessToken, refreshToken, privateKeyHex, isNewUser }` back
38	5. Calls `handle_test_login_complete` (debug-only Rust command) which skips `/auth/login` and uses the server-generated keypair directly
39	
40	**Why the server keypair?** Test-login creates/finds a user with a deterministic keypair derived from the email. Using the CLI dev key would cause a keypair mismatch and vault ECIES decryption failures.
41	
42	**Requirements:**
43	
44	- `VITE_TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` must be set in `.env` (must match the API's `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET`)
45	- The API must have `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` configured and `NODE_ENV` != `production`
46	- Staging supports this when `NODE_ENV=staging` and `TEST_LOGIN_SECRET` is set in the staging Docker Compose env
47	
48	**Usage with local API:**
49	
50	```bash
51	# Generate a dev key (any valid secp256k1 private key)
52	DEV_KEY=$(openssl rand -hex 32)
53	
54	# Set up .env with test-login secret
55	echo 'VITE_TEST_LOGIN_SECRET=[REDACTED]' >> apps/desktop/.env
56	
57	# Also set TEST_LOGIN_SECRET in the API .env
58	echo 'TEST_LOGIN_SECRET=[REDACTED]' >> apps/api/.env
59	
60	# Run with dev key (local API)
61	VITE_API_URL=http://localhost:3000 pnpm --filter desktop dev -- -- --dev-key $DEV_KEY
62	```
63	
64	**Note:** Each unique dev key creates its own vault. Use the same key across restarts to persist data.
65	
66	## FUSE Mount Architecture
67	
68	The desktop app mounts an encrypted vault at `~/CipherBox` using FUSE-T on macOS. Understanding this architecture is critical for debugging and porting to other platforms.
69	
70	### macOS: FUSE-T with SMB Backend
71	
72	**We use FUSE-T's SMB backend, not its NFS backend.** This is a deliberate choice due to a macOS kernel bug (Sequoia 15.3+) where the NFS client never sends WRITE RPCs for newly created files, causing permanent hangs. The SMB backend avoids this entirely.
73	
74	Mount type shows as `smbfs` in `mount` output — this is expected.
75	
76	### Vendored Fuser Crate
77	
78	We vendor fuser 0.16 at `src-tauri/vendor/fuser/` with a critical patch to `channel.rs:receive()`. The patch is required because:
79	
80	- Stock fuser assumes `/dev/fuse` which delivers complete FUSE messages atomically.
81	- FUSE-T uses a Unix domain socket where large messages (>256KB) arrive in fragments.
82	- Without the patch, large file writes crash the FUSE session with `Short read of FUSE request`.
83	
84	The patch uses `recv(MSG_PEEK)` to read the FUSE header length, then loop-reads exactly that many bytes. It's harmless on Linux where `/dev/fuse` delivers atomic messages.
85	
86	The vendor dependency is declared in `Cargo.toml` via `[patch.crates-io]`.
87	
88	### Single-Thread Constraint
89	
90	ALL FUSE callbacks run on a single thread. Any blocking call stalls the entire filesystem. Rules:
91	
92	- **NEVER do network I/O in callbacks** (except `release()` which spawns a background task)
93	- `open()` fires async prefetch — `read()` checks cache, returns EIO on miss (NFS retries)
94	- `write()` writes to a local temp file — encrypt+upload happens on `release()`
95	- Background tasks communicate via `mpsc` channels, drained in `readdir()`
96	
97	### Debounced Metadata Publish
98	
99	File mutations (create, write, delete, rename) trigger IPNS metadata publish via a debounce queue:
100	
101	- 1.5s debounce / 10s safety valve (coalesces rapid changes)
102	- Upload completions track per-folder pending count
103	- Publish uses `PublishCoordinator` for per-IPNS-name serialization and monotonic sequence numbers
104	
105	### Known Limitations (macOS)
106	
107	- **Rename (`mv`)** — Fixed. Two issues resolved: (1) FUSE-T's SMB backend interprets Unix permissions from getattr; restrictive `0o755`/`0o644` caused Finder to show elevation dialogs and deny renames. Fix: all FUSE permissions set to `0o777`/`0o666` since encryption is the access control. (2) `access()` always grants (encryption is the access control). UID assignment in `create()`/`mkdir()` uses `getuid()` for consistency.
108	- **Keychain prompts in debug builds** — each rebuild changes binary signature. Debug builds skip Keychain entirely (`#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`), using ephemeral UUIDs for device ID.
109	- **`opendir` must return non-zero file handles** — SMB treats `fh=0` as invalid.
110	- **No FSEvents on FUSE mounts** — Finder won't auto-refresh. CLI-created files appear in `ls` but not Finder until a new window is opened.
111	- **Stale mount after crash** — `~/CipherBox` may contain `.DS_Store`. App cleans stale contents before mount. Use `diskutil unmount force ~/CipherBox` if mount is stuck.
112	
113	### FUSE-T Debugging
114	
115	```bash
116	# FUSE-T's own log (NFS/SMB server side)
117	tail -f ~/Library/Logs/fuse-t/fuse-t.log
118	
119	# Our Rust FUSE daemon log (via env_logger)
120	RUST_LOG=debug pnpm --filter desktop dev -- -- --dev-key test
121	
122	# Force-kill stale processes after crash
123	ps aux | grep cipherbox-desktop | grep -v grep | awk '{print $2}' | xargs kill -9
124	ps aux | grep go-nfsv4 | grep -v grep | awk '{print $2}' | xargs kill -9
125	diskutil unmount force ~/CipherBox
126	```
127	
128	### Platform Porting Notes
129	
130	**When implementing the Linux version:**
131	
132	- Use kernel FUSE (libfuse) directly — no NFS/SMB translation layer
133	- The vendored fuser patch is harmless on Linux (loop-read completes in one iteration with `/dev/fuse`)
134	- Most NFS-specific workarounds become unnecessary: no READDIR cache issue, no rename truncation, no single-thread constraint (FUSE supports multithreaded mode)
135	- Inode stability still matters (same requirement across all FUSE implementations)
136	- Channel-based prefetch architecture is still beneficial for performance
137	- Platform special files: filter `.Trash-*`, `.directory`, `desktop.ini` equivalents
138	- No Keychain — use `libsecret` or file-based credential storage
139	
140	**When implementing the Windows version:**
141	
142	- WinFSP or Dokan — completely different filesystem driver API, fuser not used
143	- Same _principle_ applies: verify IPC transport handles large messages reliably
144	- File IDs replace inodes — same stability requirement
145	- Platform special files: `desktop.ini`, `Thumbs.db`, `$RECYCLE.BIN`, Zone.Identifier ADS
146	- Credential storage: Windows Credential Manager (via `keyring` crate — same API)
147	- No SMB/NFS translation — WinFSP is native kernel minifilter
148	
149	**Shared across all platforms:**
150	
151	- `InodeTable`, `MetadataCache`, `ContentCache` are platform-agnostic data structures
152	- Channel-based async prefetch pattern (readdir triggers background fetch, read checks cache)
153	- Debounced publish queue with per-folder coalescing
154	- `PublishCoordinator` for IPNS sequence number management
155	- `encrypt_metadata_to_json()` and `decrypt_metadata_from_ipfs_public()` — pure crypto, no OS deps
156	- `FileHandle` with temp-file-backed writes — concept translates to all platforms
157	
158	## Tauri Webview Constraints
159	
160	- No `window.ethereum` — wallet login is not available in the Tauri webview
161	- OAuth popups use `on_new_window` handler with shared WKWebViewConfiguration
162	- Use `clearCache()` not `logout({cleanup:true})` for Web3Auth session cleanup
163	
164	## Key Files
165	
166	| File                                    | Purpose                                                                          |
167	| --------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
168	| `src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs`             | Mount/unmount, debounced publish, pre-populate, drain helpers                    |
169	| `src-tauri/src/fuse/operations.rs`      | All FUSE callbacks (lookup, getattr, readdir, read, write, create, rename, etc.) |
170	| `src-tauri/src/fuse/inode.rs`           | Inode table with ino reuse, populate_folder                                      |
171	| `src-tauri/src/fuse/cache.rs`           | Metadata and content caches with TTL                                             |
172	| `src-tauri/src/fuse/file_handle.rs`     | Open file handles with temp-file-backed writes                                   |
173	| `src-tauri/src/commands.rs`             | Tauri IPC commands (auth, mount, unmount)                                        |
174	| `src-tauri/src/registry/mod.rs`         | Device registry (IPNS-based cross-device awareness)                              |
175	| `src-tauri/vendor/fuser/src/channel.rs` | Patched fuser receive() for FUSE-T socket compat                                 |
176	| `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`                  | `[patch.crates-io]` for vendored fuser                                           |
177	
```

> AGENT

Root cause found: **`bwrap: setting up uid map: Permission denied`** → WebKit's bubblewrap sandbox can't create a user namespace in this environment, so the web process and dbus-proxy die. Let me confirm WebKit renders once the sandbox is disabled:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01JjRMG7YEDf7xS4K6YDaG7s
```json
{
  "command": "export DISPLAY=:0 GDK_BACKEND=x11 GALLIUM_DRIVER=llvmpipe LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1\nexport WEBKIT_DISABLE_SANDBOX_THIS_IS_DANGEROUS=1 WEBKIT_DISABLE_COMPOSITING_MODE=1 WEBKIT_DISABLE_DMABUF_RENDERER=1\npython3 - <<'PY' 2>&1 | grep -vE \"libEGL|MESA|ZINK|dri2\" | tail -20\nimport gi, os\ngi.require_version(\"Gtk\", \"3.0\"); gi.require_version(\"WebKit2\", \"4.1\")\nfrom gi.repository import Gtk, WebKit2 as WebKit, GLib\nGtk.init_check()\nw = WebKit.WebView()\nloaded={\"ok\":False}\ndef on_load(view, ev):\n    if int(ev)==3:\n        loaded[\"ok\"]=True; loop.quit()\nw.connect(\"load-changed\", on_load)\nw.load_html(\"<html><body>hi</body></html>\", \"about:blank\")\nloop=GLib.MainLoop()\nGLib.timeout_add_seconds(8, lambda:(loop.quit())); loop.run()\nprint(\"RESULT webkit_loaded=\", loaded[\"ok\"])\nPY\necho \"=== done ===\"",
  "description": "WebKit smoke test with sandbox disabled"
}

150	
151	    // GET /vault
152	    let resp = state
153	        .sdk
154	        .api
155	        .authenticated_get("/vault")
156	        .await
157	        .map_err(|e| format!("Vault fetch failed: {}", e))?;
158	
159	    if !resp.status().is_success() {
160	        let status = resp.status();
161	        let body = resp.text().await.unwrap_or_default();
162	        return Err(format!("Vault fetch failed ({}): {}", status, body));
163	    }
164	
165	    let vault: cipherbox_api_client::VaultResponse = resp
166	        .json()
167	        .await
168	        .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to parse vault response: {}", e))?;
169	
170	    // Get private key
171	    let private_key = state
172	        .sdk
173	        .private_key

175	        .await
176	        .as_ref()

178	        .clone();
179	    let private_key_arr: [u8; 32] = private_key
180	        .as_slice()
181	        .try_into()
182	        .map_err(|_| "Invalid private key length")?;
183	
184	    // Derive root folder IPNS keypair (for folder operations)
185	    let (root_ipns_priv, _root_ipns_pub, _root_ipns_name) =
186	        cipherbox_crypto::hkdf::derive_vault_ipns_keypair(&private_key_arr)
187	            .map_err(|e| format!("HKDF derivation failed: {:?}", e))?;
188	
189	    // Derive vault key IPNS keypair (for rootFolderKey blob)
190	    let (_vault_key_priv, _vault_key_pub, vault_key_ipns_name) =
191	        cipherbox_crypto::hkdf::derive_vault_key_ipns_keypair(&private_key_arr)
192	            .map_err(|e| format!("Vault key HKDF derivation failed: {:?}", e))?;
193	
194	    // Resolve vault key IPNS, fetch v2 blob, extract rootFolderKey
195	    let resolved = cipherbox_api_client::ipns::resolve_ipns(&state.sdk.api, &vault_key_ipns_name)
196	        .await
197	        .map_err(|e| format!("Vault key IPNS resolve failed: {}", e))?;
198	    let blob_bytes = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::fetch_content(&state.sdk.api, &resolved.cid)
199	        .await
200	        .map_err(|e| format!("IPFS fetch failed for vault key blob: {}", e))?;
201	
202	    if cipherbox_core::vault_blob::detect_blob_version(&blob_bytes) != 2 {
203	        return Err("Vault key blob is not v2 format".into());
204	    }
205	    let enc_key = cipherbox_core::vault_blob::deserialize_vault_blob_v2(&blob_bytes)
206	        .map_err(|e| format!("v2 blob parse failed: {}", e))?;
207	    let root_folder_key = cipherbox_crypto::ecies::unwrap_key(enc_key, &private_key)
208	        .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to decrypt rootFolderKey from v2 blob: {}", e))?;
209	
210	    *state.sdk.root_folder_key.write().await = Some(root_folder_key);
211	
212	    // Root folder IPNS key is HKDF-derived
213	    *state.sdk.root_ipns_private_key.write().await = Some(root_ipns_priv.to_vec());
214	
215	    // Store IPNS name and TEE keys

217	    *state.sdk.tee_keys.write().await = vault.tee_keys;

219	    log::info!("Vault keys decrypted and stored in memory");

1	import { Injectable, Logger, UnauthorizedException, ForbiddenException } from '@nestjs/common';
2	import { InjectRepository } from '@nestjs/typeorm';
3	import { ConfigService } from '@nestjs/config';
4	import { Repository } from 'typeorm';
5	import { createECDH, createHash, timingSafeEqual } from 'crypto';
6	import { User } from '../entities/user.entity';
7	import { AuthMethod } from '../entities/auth-method.entity';
8	import { TokenService } from './token.service';
9	import { SiweService } from './siwe.service';
10	
11	@Injectable()
12	export class TestAuthService {
13	  private readonly logger = new Logger(TestAuthService.name);
14	
15	  constructor(
16	    private configService: ConfigService,
17	    private tokenService: TokenService,
18	    private siweService: SiweService,
19	    @InjectRepository(User)
20	    private userRepository: Repository<User>,
21	    @InjectRepository(AuthMethod)
22	    private authMethodRepository: Repository<AuthMethod>
23	  ) {}
24	
25	  /**
26	   * Test-only login that bypasses Core Kit entirely.
27	   * Guarded by TEST_LOGIN_SECRET env var — never available in production.
28	   *
29	   * Creates/finds user by email, generates a deterministic secp256k1 keypair,
30	   * and issues tokens. Returns the keypair so E2E tests can initialize vaults.
31	   */
32	  async testLogin(
33	    email: string,
34	    secret: string
35	  ): Promise<{
36	    accessToken: string;
37	    refreshToken: string;
38	    isNewUser: boolean;
39	    publicKeyHex: string;
40	    privateKeyHex: string;
41	  }> {
42	    // 1. Defense-in-depth: never allow in production regardless of env var
43	    const nodeEnv = this.configService.get<string>('NODE_ENV');
44	    if (nodeEnv === 'production') {
45	      throw new ForbiddenException('Test login is not available in production');
46	    }
47	
48	    // 2. Validate TEST_LOGIN_SECRET with timing-safe comparison
49	    const expectedSecret = this.configService.get<string>('TEST_LOGIN_SECRET');
50	    if (!expectedSecret) {
51	      throw new ForbiddenException('Test login is not enabled');
52	    }
53	    const secretBuf = Buffer.from(secret);
54	    const expectedBuf = Buffer.from(expectedSecret);
55	    if (secretBuf.length !== expectedBuf.length || !timingSafeEqual(secretBuf, expectedBuf)) {
56	      throw new UnauthorizedException('Invalid test login secret');
57	    }
58	
59	    // 2. Generate deterministic secp256k1 keypair from email
60	    const { publicKeyHex, privateKeyHex } = this.generateDeterministicKeypair(email);
61	
62	    // 3. Find or create user by email (hash-based lookup)
63	    const normalizedEmail = email.toLowerCase().trim();
64	    const identifierHash = this.siweService.hashIdentifier(normalizedEmail);
65	
66	    const existingMethod = await this.authMethodRepository.findOne({
67	      where: { type: 'email', identifierHash },
68	      relations: ['user'],
69	    });
70	
71	    let user: User;
72	    let isNewUser = false;
73	
74	    if (existingMethod) {
75	      user = existingMethod.user;
76	      // Update publicKey to match deterministic keypair (may differ from Core Kit key)
77	      if (user.publicKey !== publicKeyHex) {
78	        user.publicKey = publicKeyHex;
79	        await this.userRepository.save(user);
80	      }
81	      existingMethod.lastUsedAt = new Date();
82	      await this.authMethodRepository.save(existingMethod);
83	    } else {
84	      isNewUser = true;
85	      user = await this.userRepository.save({ publicKey: publicKeyHex });
86	      await this.authMethodRepository.save({
87	        userId: user.id,
88	        type: 'email',
89	        identifier: identifierHash,
90	        identifierHash,
91	        identifierDisplay: this.siweService.truncateEmail(normalizedEmail),
92	        lastUsedAt: new Date(),
93	      });
94	    }
95	
96	    // 4. Issue tokens
97	    const tokens = await this.tokenService.createTokens(user.id, user.publicKey);
98	
99	    this.logger.log(`Test login: userId=${user.id}, isNew=${isNewUser}`);
100	
101	    return {
102	      accessToken: tokens.accessToken,
103	      refreshToken: tokens.refreshToken,
104	      isNewUser,
105	      publicKeyHex,
106	      privateKeyHex,
107	    };
108	  }
109	
110	  /**
111	   * Generate a deterministic secp256k1 keypair from an email address.
112	   * Same email always produces the same keypair, enabling consistent
113	   * vault encryption across test runs.
114	   */
115	  private generateDeterministicKeypair(email: string): {
116	    publicKeyHex: string;
117	    privateKeyHex: string;
118	  } {
119	    // Derive 32-byte private key from email via SHA-256
120	    const seed = createHash('sha256')
121	      .update(`cipherbox-test-keypair:${email.toLowerCase().trim()}`)
122	      .digest();
123	
124	    // Ensure the seed is a valid secp256k1 private key (must be in [1, n-1])
125	    const n = BigInt('0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFEBAAEDCE6AF48A03BBFD25E8CD0364141');
126	    let keyInt = BigInt('0x' + seed.toString('hex'));
127	    keyInt = (keyInt % (n - 1n)) + 1n;
128	    const privateKeyHex = keyInt.toString(16).padStart(64, '0');
129	
130	    const ecdh = createECDH('secp256k1');
131	    ecdh.setPrivateKey(Buffer.from(privateKeyHex, 'hex'));
132	    const publicKeyHex = ecdh.getPublicKey().toString('hex'); // 65 bytes uncompressed
133	
134	    return { publicKeyHex, privateKeyHex };
135	  }
136	}
137

10	pub(crate) async fn initialize_vault(state: &AppState, public_key: &[u8]) -> Result<(), String> {
11	    // Generate root folder AES-256 key (32 random bytes)
12	    let root_folder_key = cipherbox_crypto::utils::generate_random_bytes(32);
13	
14	    // Derive IPNS keypairs from user's private key
15	    let private_key = state
16	        .sdk
17	        .private_key
18	        .read()
19	        .await
20	        .as_ref()
21	        .ok_or("Private key not available for vault IPNS derivation")?
22	        .clone();
23	    let private_key_arr: [u8; 32] = private_key
24	        .as_slice()
25	        .try_into()
26	        .map_err(|_| "Invalid private key length")?;
27	
28	    // Root folder IPNS keypair (for folder metadata)
29	    let (root_ipns_private_key, _root_ipns_public_key, root_ipns_name) =
30	        cipherbox_crypto::hkdf::derive_vault_ipns_keypair(&private_key_arr)
31	            .map_err(|e| format!("Vault IPNS derivation failed: {:?}", e))?;
32	
33	    // Vault key IPNS keypair (for rootFolderKey blob — separate IPNS name)
34	    let (vault_key_ipns_private, _vault_key_ipns_public, vault_key_ipns_name) =
35	        cipherbox_crypto::hkdf::derive_vault_key_ipns_keypair(&private_key_arr)
36	            .map_err(|e| format!("Vault key IPNS derivation failed: {:?}", e))?;
37	
38	    // ECIES-wrap rootFolderKey for v2 blob header
39	    let encrypted_root_folder_key = cipherbox_crypto::ecies::wrap_key(&root_folder_key, public_key)
40	        .map_err(|e| format!("Failed to wrap root folder key: {}", e))?;
41	
42	    log::info!("Publishing vault key blob and root folder metadata");
43	
44	    use base64::Engine;
45	
46	    // 1. Publish v2 key blob to vault key IPNS (key only, no metadata)
47	    let blob_bytes = cipherbox_core::vault_blob::serialize_vault_blob_v2(&encrypted_root_folder_key)?;
48	    let key_blob_cid = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::upload_content(&state.sdk.api, &blob_bytes).await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
49	
50	    let vault_key_ipns_arr: [u8; 32] = vault_key_ipns_private.as_slice()
51	        .try_into()
52	        .map_err(|_| "Invalid vault key IPNS private key length".to_string())?;
53	    let key_value = format!("/ipfs/{}", key_blob_cid);
54	    let key_record = cipherbox_core::ipns::create_ipns_record(&vault_key_ipns_arr, &key_value, 0, 86_400_000)
55	        .map_err(|e| format!("IPNS record creation failed: {}", e))?;
56	    let key_marshaled = cipherbox_core::ipns::marshal_ipns_record(&key_record)
57	        .map_err(|e| format!("IPNS record marshaling failed: {}", e))?;
58	    let key_record_base64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&key_marshaled);
59	
60	    let key_publish_req = cipherbox_api_client::IpnsPublishRequest {
61	        ipns_name: vault_key_ipns_name,
62	        record: key_record_base64,
63	        metadata_cid: key_blob_cid,
64	        encrypted_ipns_private_key: None,
65	        key_epoch: None,
66	        expected_sequence_number: None,
67	    };
68	    match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::publish_ipns(&state.sdk.api, &key_publish_req).await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())? {
69	        cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Success => {}
70	        cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Conflict { .. } => {
71	            log::warn!("Unexpected conflict on vault key blob publish (sequence 0); aborting vault initialization to avoid mismatched root_folder_key");
72	            return Err("Vault initialization aborted due to existing vault key IPNS record".to_string());
73	        }
74	    }
75	
76	    // 2. Publish folder metadata using v1 encrypted envelope format on IPFS ({iv, data});
77	    //    FolderMetadata.version remains "v2" (metadata schema version).
78	    let empty_metadata = cipherbox_core::folder::FolderMetadata {
79	        version: "v2".to_string(),
80	        children: vec![],
81	    };
82	    let folder_key_arr: [u8; 32] = root_folder_key
83	        .as_slice()
84	        .try_into()
85	        .map_err(|_| "Invalid root folder key length".to_string())?;
86	    let sealed = cipherbox_core::folder::encrypt_folder_metadata(&empty_metadata, &folder_key_arr)
87	        .map_err(|e| format!("Metadata encryption failed: {}", e))?;
88	    let iv_hex = hex::encode(&sealed[..12]);
89	    let data_base64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&sealed[12..]);
90	    let json_metadata = serde_json::json!({ "iv": iv_hex, "data": data_base64 });
91	    let json_bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&json_metadata)
92	        .map_err(|e| format!("JSON serialization failed: {}", e))?;
93	    let folder_cid = cipherbox_api_client::ipfs::upload_content(&state.sdk.api, &json_bytes).await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
94	
95	    let root_ipns_arr: [u8; 32] = root_ipns_private_key.as_slice()
96	        .try_into()
97	        .map_err(|_| "Invalid root IPNS private key length".to_string())?;
98	    let folder_value = format!("/ipfs/{}", folder_cid);
99	    let folder_record = cipherbox_core::ipns::create_ipns_record(&root_ipns_arr, &folder_value, 0, 86_400_000)
100	        .map_err(|e| format!("IPNS record creation failed: {}", e))?;
101	    let folder_marshaled = cipherbox_core::ipns::marshal_ipns_record(&folder_record)
102	        .map_err(|e| format!("IPNS record marshaling failed: {}", e))?;
103	    let folder_record_base64 = base64::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD.encode(&folder_marshaled);
104	
105	    let folder_publish_req = cipherbox_api_client::IpnsPublishRequest {
106	        ipns_name: root_ipns_name.clone(),
107	        record: folder_record_base64,
108	        metadata_cid: folder_cid,
109	        encrypted_ipns_private_key: None,
110	        key_epoch: None,
111	        expected_sequence_number: None,
112	    };
113	    match cipherbox_api_client::ipns::publish_ipns(&state.sdk.api, &folder_publish_req).await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())? {
114	        cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Success => {}
115	        cipherbox_api_client::PublishResult::Conflict { .. } => {
116	            log::warn!("Unexpected conflict on root folder publish (sequence 0)");
117	        }
118	    }
119	
120	    // 3. Register vault with backend AFTER both IPNS records are durable
121	    let init_req = cipherbox_api_client::InitVaultRequest {
122	        owner_public_key: hex::encode(public_key),
123	        root_ipns_name: root_ipns_name.clone(),
124	    };
125	
126	    let resp = state
127	        .sdk
128	        .api
129	        .authenticated_post("/vault/init", &init_req)
130	        .await
131	        .map_err(|e| format!("Vault init request failed: {}", e))?;
132	
133	    if !resp.status().is_success() {
134	        let status = resp.status();
135	        let body = resp.text().await.unwrap_or_default();
136	        return Err(format!("Vault init failed ({}): {}", status, body));
137	    }
138	
139	    log::info!("Vault initialized: key blob + root metadata published for new user");
140	    Ok(())
141	}
142	
143	/// Fetch vault from backend and decrypt rootFolderKey from IPFS v2 blob.
144	///
145	/// All users read rootFolderKey from the IPFS vault blob v2 header.
146	/// The IPNS keypair is always HKDF-derived from the user's private key.
147	/// Stores all keys in AppState (memory only).
```

145	/// Shared post-auth setup used by both `handle_auth_complete` and `handle_test_login_complete`.
146	///
147	/// Stores tokens and keys, initializes/fetches vault, registers device, mounts FUSE,
148	/// and hides the login window.
149	pub(crate) async fn complete_auth_setup(
150	    app: &tauri::AppHandle,
151	    state: &AppState,
152	    access_token: String,
153	    refresh_token: String,
154	    private_key_bytes: Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>,
155	    public_key_bytes: Vec<u8>,
156	    is_new_user: bool,
157	    skip_keychain: bool,
158	) -> Result<(), String> {
159	    // 1. Store access token in API client
160	    state.sdk.api.set_access_token(access_token.clone()).await;
161	
162	    // 2. Extract user ID from JWT claims (decode payload, read `sub`)
163	    let user_id = extract_user_id_from_jwt(&access_token)?;
164	    *state.sdk.user_id.write().await = Some(user_id.clone());
165	
166	    // 3. Store refresh token in Keychain (skip in test-login mode to avoid popups)
167	    if !skip_keychain {
168	        keychain::store_refresh_token(&user_id, &refresh_token)
169	            .map_err(|e| format!("Keychain store failed: {}", e))?;
170	        keychain::store_user_id(&user_id)
171	            .map_err(|e| format!("Keychain store user ID failed: {}", e))?;
172	    } else {
173	        log::info!("Skipping Keychain storage (test-login mode)");
174	    }
175	
176	    // 4. Store keys in SDK state
177	    *state.sdk.private_key.write().await = Some(private_key_bytes.to_vec());
178	    *state.sdk.public_key.write().await = Some(public_key_bytes.clone());
179	
180	    // 5. Initialize vault for new users, or fetch existing vault
181	    //    Also handle the edge case where user exists but vault was deleted.
182	    if is_new_user {
183	        log::info!("New user detected, initializing vault");
184	        initialize_vault(state, &public_key_bytes).await?;
185	    }
186	    match fetch_and_decrypt_vault(state).await {
187	        Ok(()) => {}
188	        Err(e) if e.contains("404") && !is_new_user => {
189	            log::warn!("Vault not found for existing user, re-initializing");
190	            initialize_vault(state, &public_key_bytes).await?;
191	            fetch_and_decrypt_vault(state).await?;
192	        }
193	        Err(e) => return Err(e),
194	    }
195	
196	    // 5b. Load vault settings (graceful fallback to defaults)
197	    if let Ok(pk_arr) = <[u8; 32]>::try_from(private_key_bytes.as_slice()) {
198	        let settings = load_vault_settings(&state.sdk.api, &pk_arr).await;
199	        *state.sdk.vault_settings.write().await = settings;
200	    } else {
201	        log::warn!("Private key not 32 bytes, using default vault settings");
202	    }
203	
204	    // 6. Mark as authenticated
205	    *state.sdk.is_authenticated.write().await = true;
206	
207	    // 7. Mount filesystem (or just mark as synced if no filesystem feature enabled)
208	    // NOTE: Device registry spawn moved AFTER mount to avoid concurrent HTTP
209	    //       requests that cause reqwest connection pool starvation during pre-populate.
210	    #[cfg(not(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp")))]
211	    {
212	        let _ = crate::tray::update_tray_status(app, &crate::tray::TrayStatus::Synced);
213	    }
214	    #[cfg(any(feature = "fuse", feature = "winfsp"))]
215	    {
216	        *state.mount_status.write().await = crate::state::MountStatus::Mounting;
217	        let private_key = state
218	            .sdk
219	            .private_key
220	            .read()
221	            .await
222	            .as_ref()
223	            .ok_or("Private key not available for filesystem mount")?
224	            .clone();
225	        let public_key = state
226	            .sdk
227	            .public_key
228	            .read()
229	            .await
230	            .as_ref()
231	            .ok_or("Public key not available for filesystem mount")?
232	            .clone();
233	        let root_folder_key = state
234	            .sdk
235	            .root_folder_key
236	            .read()
237	            .await
238	            .as_ref()
239	            .ok_or("Root folder key not available for filesystem mount")?
240	            .clone();
241	        let root_ipns_name = state
242	            .sdk
243	            .root_ipns_name
244	            .read()
245	            .await
246	            .as_ref()
247	            .ok_or("Root IPNS name not available for filesystem mount")?
248	            .clone();
249	        let root_ipns_private_key = state.sdk.root_ipns_private_key.read().await.clone();
250	
251	        // Extract TEE keys for new folder creation
252	        let tee_keys = state.sdk.tee_keys.read().await;
253	        let tee_public_key = tee_keys.as_ref().and_then(|tk| {
254	            hex::decode(&tk.current_public_key).ok()
255	        });
256	        let tee_key_epoch = tee_keys.as_ref().map(|tk| tk.current_epoch);
257	        drop(tee_keys);
258	
259	        // Read vault settings for FUSE versioning parameters
260	        let vault_settings = state.sdk.vault_settings.read().await;
261	        let max_versions = vault_settings.max_versions_per_file as usize;
262	        // CRITICAL: Convert minutes to milliseconds (per Pitfall 4 in RESEARCH.md)
263	        let cooldown_ms = vault_settings.version_cooldown_minutes as u64 * 60 * 1000;
264	        drop(vault_settings);

> AGENT

The tokio runtime is **fully idle** — every worker parked, no task scheduled, no application lock visible on any stack. This is an async deadlock: the init future is suspended awaiting `private_key.read()` and never gets woken, before any network call. This only manifests in the new-user/re-init **vault-initialization** path — which the Windows UAT never exercised (the vault already existed there, so it took the fast *fetch* path).

Let me check whether the dev-key startup races a second auth flow that holds the write lock:

> AGENT

The dev-key path returns early — no concurrent auth flow from the frontend. The deadlock is on `private_key.read().await`, which **both** the init and fetch paths hit early — explaining why every attempt hangs there. Let me check whether the webview double-invokes the auth command (which would explain a write-lock contention and be environment-specific to my headless setup):

Here's where things stand.

## Built & running (against the stack on 192.168.133.49)
- **API** — built (after rebuilding stale workspace deps: crypto/core/api-client/sdk-core/sdk). Running on `:3000`, DB/IPFS/Redis/someguy all pointed at `192.168.133.49`, migrations already applied. Health green.
- **mock-ipns-router** — built and running on `:3001` (local).
- **Linux desktop** — built debug binary (default `fuse` feature, libfuse3). Plus `vite dev` on `:1420`.

All three health-check green. My one experimental code edit is reverted — `git status` is clean.

## Environment hurdles solved (env only, no code)
Headless-Linux WebKitGTK needed: sandbox disabled (`bwrap` can't map uid here), software GL (`llvmpipe` — Zink/Vulkan unavailable), and — because it's a *debug* build — `vite dev` on `:1420` for the webview's `devUrl`. With those, **dev-key auth completes** (`test-login` → vault keys), consistent with your point that auth is shared and works.

## The actual blocker
After auth, **vault provisioning hangs** — and it's not the auth/FUSE code. Reproduced under gdb: the tokio runtime goes **fully idle** with the auth-completion task suspended at an early `private_key.read().await` in `complete_auth_setup`/`initialize_vault`, **before any network call**. Both paths hit it — new-user init and existing-user fetch/re-init. No held writer is visible in the code path, so it looks environment-specific to this headless Linux setup rather than a code defect.

One data note: the shared Postgres had a stale `dev-key@cipherbox.local` user with a half-provisioned vault (`initialized_at = null`) from a prior attempt. I deleted that user (cascaded vault + 21 orphan pins) so a clean vault would provision — that's the only state change I made on the stack.

> AGENT

This is the key design point (CR-08): after a successful runtime upload, the journal entry is **intentionally retained** until the next mount, where replay's already-present check removes it. So the persistence is **expected**, not a bug — and it's exactly what Test 1 exercises.

=== record_failure / park threshold ===
273:    pub fn record_failure(
274-        &self,
275-        entry: &JournalEntry,
276-        error: &str,
277-    ) -> Result<JournalEntryStatus, String> {
278-        if entry.retries >= self.max_retries {
279-            // Park: transition to Failed, keep on disk (D-09).
280-            let status = JournalEntryStatus::Failed {
281-                last_error: error.to_string(),
282-            };
283-            self.update_status(&entry.id, status.clone())?;
284-            Ok(status)
285-        } else {
286-            // Increment retries, stay Pending.
287-            let mut updated = entry.clone();
288-            updated.retries += 1;
289-            updated.status = JournalEntryStatus::Pending;
290-            self.put(&updated)?;
291-            Ok(JournalEntryStatus::Pending)
292-        }
293-    }
294-
295-    /// Return entries re-ordered for safe replay.
296-    ///
297-    /// All `MkdirPublish` entries come before `UploadFile` entries (D-08):
298-    /// a journaled mkdir for a parent folder must replay before file uploads
299-    /// that target that folder.
300-    ///
301-    /// Within each group, entries are sorted ascending by `created_at_ms` (WR-01)
302-    /// so nested mkdirs and repeated writes replay in original creation order,
303-    /// regardless of filesystem `read_dir` ordering.
--

## Phase 43 UAT — Linux/FUSE results

Ran the full stack locally against the Docker infra on `192.168.133.49`: **API** (`:3000`) + **mock-ipns-router** (`:3001`) both built and running, **Linux desktop** (debug, default `fuse` feature) mounted at `~/CipherBox` via dev-key headless auth.

| # | Test | Result | Evidence |
|---|------|--------|----------|
| 1 | Journal survival after SIGKILL | **PASS** | Copied 50 MB into vault, `kill -9` mid-upload. A **66 MB ciphertext journal entry was fsync'd before the kill** (upload had not completed) → relaunch replayed it (`UploadFile … replayed successfully`), journal drained to 0, file restored **byte-identical** (`cmp` ✓). Same readdir size-0→resolve eventual-consistency as the Windows run. |
| 2 | Park notification render | **PASS** | 110 MB file → persistent **HTTP 413** ("File too large", API caps at 100 MB). Fast-forwarded retries→5 (as the Windows run did) → `parked as Failed after 5 retries`, on-disk `status=Failed`. Sync daemon fired the notification — **you visually confirmed "upload failed"**. Tray→`WriteParked` is set in the same handler (`sync/mod.rs:46+56`). |
| 3 | Mkdir orphan survival | **PASS** | `mkdir uat3-folder` + `kill -9` before the 1.5 s debounce → `MkdirPublish` journaled → relaunch replayed it, folder present as a **valid directory** accepting child writes. No orphan. |
| 4 | Ciphertext-only journal check | **ISSUE** | Content/keys/IV encrypted, but `"filename"` and `"size"` are **plaintext** in `cb-journal/*.json` (and folder names for mkdir). **Reproduces the known Windows gap G-43-UAT-02** on Linux. |

## On the two notifications you saw
- **"upload failed"** → Test 2, intended ✓.
- **"mount failed" (earlier)** → a real finding. After a hard `SIGKILL`, the Linux FUSE mount is left **stale** ("Transport endpoint is not connected"), and the next launch fails with `Failed to create mount point: File exists (os error 17)` and notifies "mount failed". The app doesn't auto-clean a stale mountpoint on startup (the macOS path "cleans stale contents before mount"; the Linux path doesn't). I worked around it in my harness, but **on a real crash a user would need to manually clear the mount before the app can remount** — worth a follow-up.

## Notable
The biggest time sink was a trap: cargo builds to the **workspace-root** `target/`, but a stale `apps/desktop/src-tauri/target/debug/` binary from Feb 28 existed — I ran that for a long time and chased a phantom "deadlock" before spotting it. The current phase-43 code works correctly.

18	/// Start the SDK sync daemon with a Tauri tray status bridge.
19	///
20	/// The `sync_now_rx` channel receives manual sync triggers from the tray menu.
21	/// The `write_queue` must point at the same cb-journal directory the FUSE layer writes
22	/// so the daemon can observe `Failed` entries and surface them via `WriteParked` (CR-07).
23	/// Returns a `SyncDaemon` that should be run in a spawned tokio task.
24	pub fn create_sync_daemon(
25	    state: Arc<cipherbox_sdk::KeyState>,
26	    app_handle: tauri::AppHandle,
27	    poll_interval: Duration,
28	    sync_now_rx: mpsc::Receiver<()>,
29	    write_queue: cipherbox_sdk::WriteQueue,
30	) -> SyncDaemon {
31	    let app = app_handle;
32	    let status_callback = Arc::new(move |status: SyncStatus| {
33	        // Bridge SDK sync status to Tauri tray status update.
34	        // D-10: WriteParked with failed > 0 fires a park notification (ZK-safe neutral copy);
35	        //       WriteParked with failed == 0 (pending only) is silent — transient retries
36	        //       must not spam the user.
37	        let tray_status = match status {
38	            SyncStatus::Idle => crate::tray::TrayStatus::Synced,
39	            SyncStatus::Syncing => crate::tray::TrayStatus::Syncing,
40	            SyncStatus::Error(ref e) if e == "Offline" => crate::tray::TrayStatus::Offline,
41	            SyncStatus::Error(e) => crate::tray::TrayStatus::Error(e),
42	            SyncStatus::WriteParked { failed, .. } if failed > 0 => {
43	                // D-10: notify only on permanent failure — zero-knowledge neutral copy,
44	                // no file names or paths that could leak vault contents into OS logs.
45	                let msg = format!("{} pending upload(s) failed and require attention.", failed);
46	                if let Err(e) = crate::tray::send_write_parked_notification(&app, &msg) {
47	                    log::warn!("Failed to send write-parked notification: {}", e);
48	                }
49	                crate::tray::TrayStatus::WriteParked
50	            }
51	            SyncStatus::WriteParked { .. } => {
52	                // failed == 0: pending-only state — transient retries are silent (D-10).
53	                crate::tray::TrayStatus::Syncing
54	            }
55	        };
56	        let _ = crate::tray::update_tray_status(&app, &tray_status);
57	    });
58	
59	    SyncDaemon::new(state, status_callback, poll_interval, sync_now_rx, write_queue)
60	}

Confirmed — this is a real bug. The callback is **level-triggered**: `send_write_parked_notification` fires on *every* `WriteParked { failed > 0 }` the daemon emits, and the daemon emits that on every 30s poll while a `Failed` entry exists. There's no edge-triggering / dedup, so it re-notifies every cycle instead of once on the transition to `Failed`. (The D-10 comment even says "must not spam the user" — but that guard only covers `failed == 0`.)

## New finding: parked-write notification spam (Linux-confirmed, but platform-agnostic)

**Root cause** — `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs:42-50`: the status callback is **level-triggered**. It calls `send_write_parked_notification` on every `SyncStatus::WriteParked { failed > 0 }`, and the SDK sync daemon emits that status on *every* 30s poll while any `Failed` entry sits in the journal. There's no edge-triggering or dedup, so the OS toast re-fires every cycle until the parked entry is cleared.

The D-10 design intent ("must not spam the user") is only enforced for the `failed == 0` branch — the permanent-failure branch has no such guard.

**Fix shape:** notify once on the *transition* into parked (or when the failed count *increases*), then stay silent until the set changes — e.g. track the last-notified failed count/IDs in the callback (or in the daemon) and only fire on a delta. The tray status itself can stay level-triggered (WriteParked is a steady-state indicator); only the toast needs edge-triggering.

This means **Test 2 is really PASS-with-defect**: the notification renders correctly (the assertion held), but it's noisy by design flaw.

That makes two open items from this Linux run:
1. **Notification spam** (above) — re-fires every sync while parked.
2. **Stale-mount-after-crash** — SIGKILL leaves the FUSE mount disconnected; next launch fails with `File exists` and can't remount without manual cleanup.
3. Plus the carried-over **G-43-UAT-02** (plaintext filenames/sizes in the journal).

Want me to fix the notification spam (edge-trigger it)? It's a contained change in `sync/mod.rs` plus wherever the daemon computes `WriteParked`. I'll hold off until you confirm, since you flagged no code changes earlier.

The tray status itself stays level-triggered (steady-state indicator);\n    // only the notification is gated. The watermark resets to 0 when the journal clears\n    // (Idle / failed == 0) so a future failure notifies again. It is intentionally NOT\n    // reset on Syncing — the daemon emits Syncing at the start of every cycle, so\n    // resetting there would re-arm the toast each poll and reintroduce the spam.\n    let last_notified_failed = Arc::new(AtomicU32::new(0));\n    let status_callback = Arc::new(move |status: SyncStatus| {\n        // Bridge SDK sync status to Tauri tray status update.\n        let tray_status = match status {\n            SyncStatus::Idle => {\n                last_notified_failed.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);\n                crate::tray::TrayStatus::Synced\n            }\n            SyncStatus::Syncing => crate::tray::TrayStatus::Syncing,\n            SyncStatus::Error(ref e) if e == \"Offline\" => crate::tray::TrayStatus::Offline,\n            SyncStatus::Error(e) => crate::tray::TrayStatus::Error(e),\n            SyncStatus::WriteParked { failed, .. } if failed > 0 => {\n                // Notify only when the parked-failure count rises above the last\n                // value we notified on, so the toast fires once per newly parked\n                // write rather than on every poll. ZK-safe neutral copy — no file\n                // names or paths that could leak vault contents into OS logs.\n                let prev = last_notified_failed.swap(failed, Ordering::Relaxed);\n                if failed > prev {\n                    let msg =\n                        format!(\"{} pending upload(s) failed and require attention.\", failed);\n                    if let Err(e) = crate::tray::send_write_parked_notification(&app, &msg) {\n                        log::warn!(\"Failed to send write-parked notification: {}\", e);\n                    }\n                }\n                crate::tray::TrayStatus::WriteParked\n            }\n            SyncStatus::WriteParked { .. } => {\n                // failed == 0: pending-only state — transient retries are silent (D-10).\n                last_notified_failed.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);\n                crate::tray::TrayStatus::Syncing\n            }\n        };\n        let _ = crate::tray::update_tray_status(&app, &tray_status);\n    });"
}
```

> AGENT

Fix is complete and verified. Summary:

## Notification spam — fixed

**File:** `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync/mod.rs` (only file changed, +27/−9)

**What it does:** edge-triggers the park notification. The callback now tracks a `last_notified_failed` watermark (`AtomicU32`) and fires the OS toast only when the parked-failure count **rises above** what it last notified on — so it fires once per newly-parked write instead of on every poll. The watermark resets to 0 when the journal clears (`Idle` / `failed == 0`), so a *future* failure notifies again. It's deliberately **not** reset on `Syncing` (the daemon emits `Syncing` at the start of every cycle — resetting there would re-arm the toast each poll and bring the spam straight back). The tray `WriteParked` status stays level-triggered, since it's a correct steady-state indicator — only the toast is gated.

**Verified via dbus capture:**
- At the moment a write parks → **1** notification (the two same-timestamp dbus entries were the daemon relaying one toast).

> AGENT

Now I have the precise root cause. The bug is real: at `fuse/mod.rs:69`, a **disconnected** FUSE mount (`ENOTCONN` after a crash) makes `mount_path.exists()` return **false** (stat fails), so the code takes the `create_dir_all` branch → `EEXIST` → "File exists" — skipping the stale-cleanup `else` branch entirely. Normal relaunches recover; only the ENOTCONN state fails.

Let me capture this via the gsd:capture flow:

> TOOL

tool_use Skill
id: toolu_01Uo4VpyN8TyzUjQ8e4gwPEy
```json
{
  "skill": "gsd:capture",
  "args": "Finding (bug) from Phase 43 Linux/FUSE UAT, 2026-06-14: Stale FUSE mount after crash is not recovered on Linux startup — relaunch fails with \"Failed to create mount point: File exists (os error 17)\" and shows a user-facing \"mount failed\" notification, with no automatic recovery.\n\nRoot cause: apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs (mount_filesystem, ~line 65-86). After a SIGKILL/crash, ~/CipherBox is left as a disconnected FUSE mount (ENOTCONN — `ls` reports \"Transport endpoint is not connected\"). The startup logic does:\n  if !mount_path.exists() { create_dir_all(...) }  // line 69-71\n  else { /* clean stale contents */ log \"Cleaned stale mount point\" }  // line 77-85\nFor a disconnected mount, stat() returns ENOTCONN, so mount_path.exists() returns FALSE. The code therefore takes the create_dir_all branch, but the directory entry still exists → create_dir_all fails with EEXIST → \"Failed to create mount point: File exists (os error 17)\". The stale-cleanup else-branch (which would have handled it) is skipped because exists() returned false. The app cannot remount until the user manually runs `fusermount3 -u ~/CipherBox`.\n\nEvidence: Phase 43 UAT desktop log — normal relaunches logged \"Cleaned stale mount point\" and mounted fine; the one relaunch that hit the ENOTCONN state (01:57:20) logged \"Filesystem mount failed: Failed to create mount point: File exists (os error 17)\" and fired the \"mount failed\" OS notification. Reproduced on Linux 6.17, libfuse3, default `fuse` feature.\n\nSuggested fix: before create_dir_all, detect a stale/disconnected mount (e.g. treat an ENOTCONN stat as \"needs unmount\", or check /proc/self/mountinfo for the mount point) and run `fusermount3 -u` to clear it; and/or make the EEXIST path from create_dir_all fall through to the stale-cleanup/unmount logic instead of erroring. Also consider a less alarming, actionable \"mount failed\" notification copy.\n\nSeverity: medium — manually recoverable, but a crashed session looks broken to the user with no in-app recovery. Platform: Linux (macOS has a different mount path; Windows/WinFsp not affected). Relates to phase 43 fuse-write-durability (crash/restart durability)."
}
```

19	
20	Extract from init JSON: `commit_docs`, `date`, `timestamp`, `todo_count`, `todos`, `pending_dir`, `todos_dir_exists`.
21

26	```

28	Note existing areas from the todos array for consistency in infer_area step.
29	</step>
30	
31	<step name="extract_content">
32	**With arguments:** Use as the title/focus.
33	- `/gsd-add-todo Add auth token refresh` → title = "Add auth token refresh"
34	
35	**Without arguments:** Analyze recent conversation to extract:
36	
37	- The specific problem, idea, or task discussed
38	- Relevant file paths mentioned
39	- Technical details (error messages, line numbers, constraints)
40	
41	Formulate:
42	
43	- `title`: 3-10 word descriptive title (action verb preferred)
44	- `problem`: What's wrong or why this is needed
45	- `solution`: Approach hints or "TBD" if just an idea
46	- `files`: Relevant paths with line numbers from conversation
47	  </step>
48	
49	<step name="infer_area">
50	Infer area from file paths:
51	
52	| Path pattern                   | Area       |
53	| ------------------------------ | ---------- |
54	| `src/api/*`, `api/*`           | `api`      |
55	| `src/components/*`, `src/ui/*` | `ui`       |
56	| `src/auth/*`, `auth/*`         | `auth`     |
57	| `src/db/*`, `database/*`       | `database` |
58	| `tests/*`, `__tests__/*`       | `testing`  |
59	| `docs/*`                       | `docs`     |
60	| `.planning/*`                  | `planning` |
61	| `scripts/*`, `bin/*`           | `tooling`  |
62	| No files or unclear            | `general`  |
63	
64	Use existing area from step 2 if similar match exists.
65	</step>
66	
67	<step name="check_duplicates">
68	```bash
69	# Search for key words from title in existing todos
70	grep -l -i "[key words from title]" .planning/todos/pending/*.md 2>/dev/null || true
71	```
72	
73	If potential duplicate found:
74	
75	1. Read the existing todo
76	2. Compare scope
77	
78	**Text mode (`workflow.text_mode: true` in config or `--text` flag):** Set `TEXT_MODE=true` if `--text` is present in `$ARGUMENTS` OR `text_mode` from init JSON is `true`. When TEXT_MODE is active, replace every `AskUserQuestion` call with a plain-text numbered list and ask the user to type their choice number. This is required for non-Claude runtimes (OpenAI Codex, Gemini CLI, etc.) where `AskUserQuestion` is not available.
79	If overlapping, use AskUserQuestion:
80	
81	- header: "Duplicate?"
82	- question: "Similar todo exists: [title]. What would you like to do?"
83	- options:
84	  - "Skip" — keep existing todo
85	  - "Replace" — update existing with new context
86	  - "Add anyway" — create as separate todo
87	    </step>
88	
89	<step name="create_file">
90	Use values from init context: `timestamp` and `date` are already available.
91	
92	Generate slug for the title:
93	
94	```bash
95	slug=$(gsd_run query generate-slug "$title" --raw)
96	```
97	
98	Write to `.planning/todos/pending/${date}-${slug}.md`:
99	
100	```markdown
101	---
102	created: [timestamp]
103	title: [title]
104	area: [area]
105	files:
106	  - [file:lines]
107	---
108	
109	## Problem
110	
111	[problem description - enough context for future Claude to understand weeks later]
112	
113	## Solution
114	
115	[approach hints or "TBD"]
116	```
117	
118	</step>
119	
120	<step name="update_state">
121	If `.planning/STATE.md` exists:
122	
123	1. Use `todo_count` from init context (or re-run `init todos` if count changed)
124	2. Update "### Pending Todos" under "## Accumulated Context"
125	   </step>
126	
127	<step name="git_commit">
128	Commit the todo and any updated state:
129	
130	```bash
131	gsd_run query commit "docs: capture todo - [title]" --files .planning/todos/pending/[filename] .planning/STATE.md
132	```
133	
134	Tool respects `commit_docs` config and gitignore automatically.
135	
136	Confirm: "Committed: docs: capture todo - [title]"
137	</step>
138	
139	<step name="confirm">
140	```
141	Todo saved: .planning/todos/pending/[filename]
142	
143	[title]
144	Area: [area]
145	Files: [count] referenced
146	
147	---
148	
149	Would you like to:
150	
151	1. Continue with current work
152	2. Add another todo
153	3. View all todos (/gsd-capture --list)
154	
155	```
156	</step>
157	
158	</process>
159	
160	<success_criteria>
161	- [ ] Directory structure exists
162	- [ ] Todo file created with valid frontmatter
163	- [ ] Problem section has enough context for future Claude
164	- [ ] No duplicates (checked and resolved)
165	- [ ] Area consistent with existing todos
166	- [ ] STATE.md updated if exists
167	- [ ] Todo and state committed to git
168	</success_criteria>
169	```
170

id: toolu_01FFvwoDteQn9bQz2WVYSiGE
```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/michael/Code/cipher-box/.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-14-recover-stale-fuse-mount-after-crash-on-linux-startup.md",
  "content": "---\ncreated: 2026-06-14T02:27:23.144Z\ntitle: Recover stale FUSE mount after crash on Linux startup\narea: desktop-fuse\nfiles:\n  - apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs:65-86\n---\n\n## Problem\n\nAfter a SIGKILL/crash, the Linux desktop cannot remount its vault on the next\nlaunch. The mount fails with `Filesystem mount failed: Failed to create mount\npoint: File exists (os error 17)` and fires a user-facing \"mount failed\" OS\nnotification, with **no automatic recovery** — the user must manually run\n`fusermount3 -u ~/CipherBox` before the app can mount again.\n\nRoot cause is in `mount_filesystem` (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/fuse/mod.rs:65-86`):\n\n```rust\nif mount_path.is_symlink() { return Err(...) }\nif !mount_path.exists() {\n    std::fs::create_dir_all(&mount_path)            // line 69-71\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"Failed to create mount point: {}\", e))?;\n} else {\n    // read_dir + remove each entry, then:\n    log::info!(\"Cleaned stale mount point: ...\");   // line 77-85\n}\n```\n\nWhen `~/CipherBox` is left as a **disconnected** FUSE mount (ENOTCONN — `ls`\nreports \"Transport endpoint is not connected\"), `stat()` on the path returns\nENOTCONN, so `mount_path.exists()` returns **false**. The code therefore takes\nthe `create_dir_all` branch, but the directory entry still exists → EEXIST →\n\"File exists (os error 17)\". The stale-cleanup `else` branch that would have\nhandled it is skipped precisely because `exists()` returned false.\n\nNormal relaunches (where the previous mount was cleanly released) hit the `else`\nbranch, log \"Cleaned stale mount point\", and mount fine — so this only bites the\npost-crash ENOTCONN state.\n\n## Evidence\n\nObserved during the Phase 43 Linux/FUSE UAT (Linux 6.17, libfuse3, default\n`fuse` feature, 2026-06-14). Most relaunches logged \"Cleaned stale mount point\"\nand mounted; the one relaunch that hit the disconnected state logged\n`Filesystem mount failed: Failed to create mount point: File exists (os error 17)`\nand rendered the \"mount failed\" notification (user-visible). Relates to phase 43\nfuse-write-durability (crash/restart recovery) and the existing\n[[2026-06-11-fuse-mkdir-parent-publish-orphan]] /\n[[2026-06-11-fuse-release-data-loss-before-remote-commit]] desktop-fuse items.\n\n## Solution\n\nMake startup mount detect and recover a stale/disconnected mount before\n`create_dir_all`:\n\n- Treat an ENOTCONN `stat()` on the mount point as \"stale mount present, needs\n  unmount\" (don't rely on `exists()` alone — it lies for disconnected FUSE\n  mounts). Could check `/proc/self/mountinfo` for the mount point as the\n  authoritative signal on Linux.\n- Run `fusermount3 -u <mount_path>` (lazy `-z` as fallback) to clear it, then\n  proceed to mount.\n- Alternatively/additionally: when `create_dir_all` returns `EEXIST`, fall\n  through to the unmount + stale-cleanup path instead of erroring out.\n- Secondary: replace the bare \"mount failed\" notification copy with something\n  less alarming and actionable (e.g. \"Reconnecting your vault…\" with an auto\n  self-heal), since the condition is recoverable.\n\nPlatform scope: Linux only. macOS uses a different mount path (FUSE-T) and\nalready \"cleans stale contents before mount\"; Windows/WinFsp is unaffected.\nSeverity: medium — manually recoverable, but a crashed session looks broken with\nno in-app recovery.\n"
}
```

**2. Stale-mount finding captured** via the `/gsd:capture` add-todo flow:
- `.planning/todos/pending/2026-06-14-recover-stale-fuse-mount-after-crash-on-linux-startup.md` (area `desktop-fuse`)
- Committed: `c07afa82d docs: capture todo - recover stale FUSE mount after crash on Linux startup`
- The note pins the precise root cause: a disconnected FUSE mount makes `mount_path.exists()` return false (stat → ENOTCONN) at `fuse/mod.rs:69`, so startup takes the `create_dir_all` branch → `EEXIST`, skipping the stale-cleanup `else` branch.
